I’ve also been moving boxes into these little patterns even when people are yelling at me to stop.\n\nDoctor: Have you been seeing, hearing, or feeling things that others don’t, like voices or visions, especially when you’re doing these patterns or copying sounds?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed voices or visions, not like that. When I’m doing the patterns or copying sounds, I’m just… focused on it, I guess. I’m not sure how else to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that others don’t seem to believe or understand, like something being very important or connected in a way that others don’t see?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think I have some big belief like that. I mean, the patterns feel important when I’m doing them, but I don’t really know why. Other people don’t seem to get it, but I can’t explain it as a belief or anything.\n\nDoctor: When you’re arranging the boxes in patterns or making those sounds, do you feel like it has to be done a certain way to prevent something bad from happening, or like it means something special that other people don’t understand?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think I’m doing it to stop something bad, no. It’s more like I just get stuck doing it. Like at the warehouse I’ll freeze with my arms up by the forklift, or copy the beeping and the hand signals, or start putting boxes into these weird little patterns. People yell at me to stop, but I just keep doing it for a while. I can’t really say it means something special.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that you know other people don’t believe, even if you can’t explain why you believe it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I get stuck on things and they feel hard to let go of, but I can’t say I believe something other people don’t. I don’t really have a reason like that in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like there’s a special meaning or hidden message in the patterns you make or the sounds you copy, even if you can’t quite put it into words?\n\nPatient: Not really. I don’t notice a message in it. When I’m doing it, it feels like I’m just stuck on the pattern or the sound, like I keep following it, but I couldn’t tell you it means something. It’s not like I’m getting a hidden message from it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I don’t really have a reason like that in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like there’s a special meaning or hidden message in the patterns you make or the sounds you copy, even if you can’t quite put it into words?\n\nPatient: Not really. I don’t notice a message in it. When I’m doing it, it feels like I’m just stuck on the pattern or the sound, like I keep following it, but I couldn’t tell you it means something. It’s not like I’m getting a hidden message from it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that felt completely out of the ordinary, like something was very wrong with your mind, or that parts of your body were changing in strange ways?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, sometimes I feel like my head isn’t working right, but not like my body is changing or anything like that. I haven’t really had thoughts like that, at least not that I can explain.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any thoughts or beliefs that you know are not true or that others don’t believe, even if you can’t explain why you have them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I have anything like that. I mean, I get confused sometimes, but I can’t point to a belief and say I know it’s not true or that other people wouldn’t believe it. I haven’t noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences where you saw, heard, or felt something that wasn’t really there, or believed something was happening that others didn’t seem to notice or agree with, especially when you were doing those patterns or sounds?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t noticed seeing or hearing things that weren’t there when I’m doing the patterns or sounds. It’s more like I get caught up in doing it, but I’m not aware of something happening that other people don’t see.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I haven’t really had thoughts like that, at least not that I can explain.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any thoughts or beliefs that you know are not true or that others don’t believe, even if you can’t explain why you have them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I have anything like that. I mean, I get confused sometimes, but I can’t point to a belief and say I know it’s not true or that other people wouldn’t believe it. I haven’t noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences where you saw, heard, or felt something that wasn’t really there, or believed something was happening that others didn’t seem to notice or agree with, especially when you were doing those patterns or sounds?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t noticed seeing or hearing things that weren’t there when I’m doing the patterns or sounds. It’s more like I get caught up in doing it, but I’m not aware of something happening that other people don’t see.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any thoughts that felt completely strange or out of the blue, like someone was watching you, or that you were receiving special messages, even if you didn’t act on them or tell anyone?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had weird thoughts sometimes, but not like someone was watching me or sending me messages. Nothing that really stands out like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or ideas that you didn’t share with others because you knew they wouldn’t understand or might think they were strange, even if you didn’t act on them?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I can’t think of any ideas like that that I was hiding because people would think they were strange. I might keep some things to myself just because I don’t feel like talking, but not because I had some unusual idea I was afraid they wouldn’t understand.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when your behavior, thinking, or mood changed suddenly in a way that was very out of character for you, and that others might have noticed even if you didn’t talk about it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that’s kind of what happened at work. People said I was acting weird. I’d freeze up with my arms raised near the forklift, or I’d start copying the beeping sounds and the hand signals.\n\nSometimes I’d move boxes into these little patterns instead of just doing the job, even when people were yelling at me to stop. I didn’t really explain it to them. I think that’s part of why they brought me in.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any thoughts that you kept to yourself because you were afraid others wouldn’t understand them or might think they were too strange, even if you didn’t act on them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I keep things to myself sometimes, but not because I thought they were too strange or that people wouldn’t understand them. I can’t really think of anything like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, I get stuck on things and they feel hard to let go of, but I can’t say I believe something other people don’t. I don’t really have a reason like that in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like there’s a special meaning or hidden message in the patterns you make or the sounds you copy, even if you can’t quite put it into words?\n\nPatient: Not really. I haven’t noticed seeing or hearing things that weren’t there when I’m doing the patterns or sounds. It’s more like I get caught up in doing it, but I’m not aware of something happening that other people don’t see.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any thoughts that felt completely strange or out of the blue, like someone was watching you, or that you were receiving special messages, even if you didn’t act on them or tell anyone?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. Nothing that really stands out like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or ideas that you didn’t share with others because you knew they wouldn’t understand or might think they were strange, even if you didn’t act on them?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I can’t really think of anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a strong feeling or belief that something was true, even though you knew others didn’t share that belief or might not understand it, even if you didn’t talk about it or act on it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I can’t really point to any belief like that where I was sure it was true and nobody else agreed. Yeah. That happens to me at work sometimes. Like if my supervisor asks which pallets are supposed to go on the truck, I’ll start trying to answer, but then my words jump around. I’ll be talking about the load sheet, then somehow I’m talking about my old house, or fishing, or boxes from church. 

By the time I’m done, people are just looking at me like they don’t know what I meant. And honestly, sometimes I don’t even know how I got from one thing to the other.
